Um, yeah, hi. I came in because I feel like I just cannot keep everything organized at the same time. Like, I’m trying to do schoolwork, and then I have this little accessories shop thing too—mostly supplies and receipts and little notes everywhere—and it all turns into piles.

I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.

And then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.

Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, hi. I came in because I feel like I just cannot keep everything organized at the same time. Like, I’m trying to do schoolwork, and then I have this little accessories shop thing too—mostly supplies and receipts and little notes everywhere—and it all turns into piles.\n\nAnd then with school, due dates and assignment notes get mixed in with, like, shop stuff, and I’ll sit there not knowing what I’m supposed to do first. I actually bought these cute folders thinking that would fix it, but then I kind of just made a pile next to the folders, which is… not helpful. So yeah, I think that’s the main reason.\n\nDoctor: Are these organizational difficulties causing you a lot of stress or affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely affecting stuff. Like, I kept thinking it was just messy and annoying, but then I missed some required steps for my program—forms or something I was supposed to do—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is really stressful.\n\nAnd with the shop projects, like, I’ll be working with people on things, and I can tell they don’t trust me as much to handle certain parts anymore. Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. Like supplies, receipts, due dates, assignment notes—everything ends up in the same piles, and then I’m staring at it like, okay, what was I supposed to do first?\n\nI guess it kind of spills over because those things are most of my day anyway. Like I’ll be trying to sort school papers and then find a receipt from the shop, and then I remember an assignment, and then I’m suddenly reorganizing beads or packaging instead of doing any of it. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.

So… yeah, it’s mostly those areas, but they kind of take over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty often. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.

It’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.

So I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.

Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So yeah, I think that’s the main reason.\n\nDoctor: Are these organizational difficulties causing you a lot of stress or affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely affecting stuff. Like, I kept thinking it was just messy and annoying, but then I missed some required steps for my program—forms or something I was supposed to do—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is really stressful.\n\nAnd with the shop projects, like, I’ll be working with people on things, and I can tell they don’t trust me as much to handle certain parts anymore. Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. Like supplies, receipts, due dates, assignment notes—everything ends up in the same piles, and then I’m staring at it like, okay, what was I supposed to do first?\n\nI guess it kind of spills over because those things are most of my day anyway. Like I’ll be trying to sort school papers and then find a receipt from the shop, and then I remember an assignment, and then I’m suddenly reorganizing beads or packaging instead of doing any of it. So… yeah, it’s mostly those areas, but they kind of take over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty often. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. So yeah, it is interfering.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F90.0", "F90.8", "F90.9"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A lot, honestly. I keep trying to tell myself it’s just annoying or I’ll fix it next week, but then it turns into actual consequences.

Like with my program, I missed required steps—forms and things I was supposed to do in the right order—and now my financial aid is at risk, which makes me feel kind of sick when I think about it. And then with my shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do.

So yeah… it’s not just frustrating. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.

I actually bought these cute folders thinking that would fix it, but then I kind of just made a pile next to the folders, which is… not helpful. So yeah, I think that’s the main reason.\n\nDoctor: Are these organizational difficulties causing you a lot of stress or affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely affecting stuff. Like, I kept thinking it was just messy and annoying, but then I missed some required steps for my program—forms or something I was supposed to do—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is really stressful.\n\nAnd with the shop projects, like, I’ll be working with people on things, and I can tell they don’t trust me as much to handle certain parts anymore. Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. Like supplies, receipts, due dates, assignment notes—everything ends up in the same piles, and then I’m staring at it like, okay, what was I supposed to do first?\n\nI guess it kind of spills over because those things are most of my day anyway. Like I’ll be trying to sort school papers and then find a receipt from the shop, and then I remember an assignment, and then I’m suddenly reorganizing beads or packaging instead of doing any of it. So… yeah, it’s mostly those areas, but they kind of take over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty often. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced symptoms like disorganization, impulsivity, or restlessness for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

Yeah, I think so. My mom actually still has these old elementary school notes from teachers, which is kind of embarrassing that they still exist, but they say stuff like I was always getting out of my seat or talking before I got called on.

Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Yeah… I mean, I don’t totally remember it clearly myself, because I was little, but my mom still has these notes from elementary school. They say I was always out of my seat, talking before I got called on, and rushing through group activities even when I did understand the work.

So I guess it wasn’t always that I didn’t know what to do, it was more like I’d jump ahead or do it too fast. My mom keeps all that stuff in this box, like report cards and random class things—she has, like, every paper ever, which is kind of intense—but yeah, from the notes it sounds like teachers were bringing it up even back then.

I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. So yeah, it is interfering.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do you feel your organizational difficulties and other symptoms are causing you?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I keep trying to tell myself it’s just annoying or I’ll fix it next week, but then it turns into actual consequences.\n\nLike with my program, I missed required steps—forms and things I was supposed to do in the right order—and now my financial aid is at risk, which makes me feel kind of sick when I think about it. And then with my shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not just frustrating. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced symptoms like disorganization, impulsivity, or restlessness for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My mom actually still has these old elementary school notes from teachers, which is kind of embarrassing that they still exist, but they say stuff like I was always getting out of my seat or talking before I got called on.\n\nAnd I guess I would rush through group activities too, even if I understood the work. Like I wasn’t necessarily confused, I’d just… go too fast or jump in. My mom brings it up sometimes when she’s looking through old papers, and then she’ll start talking about how she saved everything from school, like even random art projects, which is a whole other thing. But yeah, it seems like it was there pretty young.\n\nDoctor: Did you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t totally remember it clearly myself, because I was little, but my mom still has these notes from elementary school. My mom keeps all that stuff in this box, like report cards and random class things—she has, like, every paper ever, which is kind of intense—but yeah, from the notes it sounds like teachers were bringing it up even back then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like, I’m trying to do schoolwork, and then I have this little accessories shop thing too—mostly supplies and receipts and little notes everywhere—and it all turns into piles.\n\nAnd then with school, due dates and assignment notes get mixed in with, like, shop stuff, and I’ll sit there not knowing what I’m supposed to do first. I actually bought these cute folders thinking that would fix it, but then I kind of just made a pile next to the folders, which is… not helpful. So yeah, I think that’s the main reason.\n\nDoctor: Are these organizational difficulties causing you a lot of stress or affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely affecting stuff. Like, I kept thinking it was just messy and annoying, but then I missed some required steps for my program—forms or something I was supposed to do—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is really stressful.\n\nAnd with the shop projects, like, I’ll be working with people on things, and I can tell they don’t trust me as much to handle certain parts anymore. Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. Like supplies, receipts, due dates, assignment notes—everything ends up in the same piles, and then I’m staring at it like, okay, what was I supposed to do first?\n\nI guess it kind of spills over because those things are most of my day anyway. Like I’ll be trying to sort school papers and then find a receipt from the shop, and then I remember an assignment, and then I’m suddenly reorganizing beads or packaging instead of doing any of it. So… yeah, it’s mostly those areas, but they kind of take over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty often. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. So yeah, it is interfering.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do you feel your organizational difficulties and other symptoms are causing you?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I keep trying to tell myself it’s just annoying or I’ll fix it next week, but then it turns into actual consequences.\n\nLike with my program, I missed required steps—forms and things I was supposed to do in the right order—and now my financial aid is at risk, which makes me feel kind of sick when I think about it. And then with my shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not just frustrating. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced symptoms like disorganization, impulsivity, or restlessness for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My mom actually still has these old elementary school notes from teachers, which is kind of embarrassing that they still exist, but they say stuff like I was always getting out of my seat or talking before I got called on.\n\nAnd I guess I would rush through group activities too, even if I understood the work. Like I wasn’t necessarily confused, I’d just… go too fast or jump in. My mom brings it up sometimes when she’s looking through old papers, and then she’ll start talking about how she saved everything from school, like even random art projects, which is a whole other thing. But yeah, it seems like it was there pretty young.\n\nDoctor: Did you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t totally remember it clearly myself, because I was little, but my mom still has these notes from elementary school. They say I was always out of my seat, talking before I got called on, and rushing through group activities even when I did understand the work.\n\nSo I guess it wasn’t always that I didn’t know what to do, it was more like I’d jump ahead or do it too fast. My mom keeps all that stuff in this box, like report cards and random class things—she has, like, every paper ever, which is kind of intense—but yeah, from the notes it sounds like teachers were bringing it up even back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity in all areas of your life, or are there certain situations where you seem to manage better?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of… everywhere, but it shows up differently depending on what I’m doing. Like with my program, it got really serious because I missed required steps—forms or whatever order things were supposed to happen in—and now my financial aid is at risk, which is awful.\n\nAnd with shop projects, that’s where I hate it because it affects other people. People have started depending on me less, because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do. I guess sometimes if something is really immediate or someone is standing right there, I manage better, but if it’s something I have to organize myself, it falls apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity consistently throughout your life, without any significant periods where these issues were absent?\n\nPatient: Not totally without breaks, I guess. Like, it’s been a real problem for about… two years and a couple months, since I changed schools.\n\nThat’s when I had to handle the business program stuff and my accessories shop with way less structure, and it kind of became obvious I wasn’t keeping up with organizing everything myself. Before that, I mean, things were probably there, but this level of it being a problem has really been since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think the organization and focus part is bigger for me, honestly. Like, that’s why I came in—because I feel like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.

It’s like my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes… they all just end up in piles, and then I look at it and I don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. I’ll be like, “Okay, I’m going to sort this,” and then I find a receipt from three weeks ago, and then I remember I needed beads for something, and then somehow I’m not doing the assignment either. So, yeah, the getting organized and staying on track feels like the main thing.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, hi. I came in because I feel like I just cannot keep everything organized at the same time. Like, I’m trying to do schoolwork, and then I have this little accessories shop thing too—mostly supplies and receipts and little notes everywhere—and it all turns into piles.\n\nAnd then with school, due dates and assignment notes get mixed in with, like, shop stuff, and I’ll sit there not knowing what I’m supposed to do first. I actually bought these cute folders thinking that would fix it, but then I kind of just made a pile next to the folders, which is… not helpful. So yeah, I think that’s the main reason.\n\nDoctor: Are these organizational difficulties causing you a lot of stress or affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely affecting stuff. Like, I kept thinking it was just messy and annoying, but then I missed some required steps for my program—forms or something I was supposed to do—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is really stressful.\n\nAnd with the shop projects, like, I’ll be working with people on things, and I can tell they don’t trust me as much to handle certain parts anymore. Which feels awful, because I don’t mean to be flaky, it just all gets tangled up and then suddenly there are consequences.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stay organized in other areas of your life besides school and your shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the biggest examples are definitely school and the shop, because those are the ones where there’s actual stuff everywhere. Like supplies, receipts, due dates, assignment notes—everything ends up in the same piles, and then I’m staring at it like, okay, what was I supposed to do first?\n\nI guess it kind of spills over because those things are most of my day anyway. Like I’ll be trying to sort school papers and then find a receipt from the shop, and then I remember an assignment, and then I’m suddenly reorganizing beads or packaging instead of doing any of it. So… yeah, it’s mostly those areas, but they kind of take over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty often. Like in class, I’ll start out actually listening, and then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or like, “Oh, that color combo would be cute,” or I’ll start picturing packaging for an order.\n\nSame thing if I’m watching a video for business assignments or school stuff. I’ll mean to pay attention, but then my brain kind of wanders into shop ideas, and suddenly I realize I missed, like, half of what they said. Then I have to rewind it, and sometimes I still drift off again.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot. Even if I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body is kind of… buzzing? Like I need to be moving somehow.\n\nEspecially when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, that’s when I notice it more. I’ll be sitting there thinking, okay, just be normal and stay still, but it feels really uncomfortable, like there’s this pressure to move or shift around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens. Like if a teacher gives me a list of things to turn in, I’ll do the first part and feel like, okay, I’m on it, but then later I’ll realize I missed some random important piece, like attaching the form or finishing the last section.\n\nIt happens with shop stuff too, like after a vendor meeting or with an order note. I’ll write down part of it, or start doing it, and then I get pulled into something else—like packaging or checking supplies—and then later I’m like, wait, I never finished the actual last step. It’s frustrating because I’ll think I followed it, but I kind of only followed the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble with organization and focus, or is this something that started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little, honestly. My mom still has some of my elementary school notes, like the ones teachers send home, and they’d say I was always getting out of my seat or talking before they called on me.\n\nAnd like… I understood the work most of the time, but in group activities I’d rush through things or jump ahead, and then everyone would get annoyed because I wasn’t waiting. My mom keeps everything, even old permission slips and stuff, which is kind of embarrassing, but yeah, it doesn’t feel like it just started recently.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in with my answer before the other person is even finished talking.\n\nAnd then sometimes I realize I wasn’t even responding to what they actually said, I was responding to what I thought they were about to say. Which is so embarrassing, because then I’m like, okay, great, now everyone knows I wasn’t waiting. I don’t mean to be rude, it just comes out really fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have intense mood swings or find it hard to manage your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I mean, I get annoyed or stressed like anyone, I guess, but “intense mood swings” sounds kind of big. I’m not sure if I’d call it that.\n\nLike sometimes I’ll be upset and then later I’m fine, but that also depends on what’s going on, you know? Like if someone’s being short with me, or if I’m already tired, or if plans change—actually plans changing is so annoying, because then I start thinking about all the stuff I had set up in my head, and it’s like, okay, now what am I supposed to do with the rest of the day?\n\nBut yeah… I don’t know if that counts as mood swings. I haven’t really tracked it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization and staying on task are more of a problem for you than your impulsivity or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think the organization part is honestly the thing that made me come in. Like, the impulsive stuff is embarrassing, but right now it’s more like I can’t keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time.\n\nI’ll have supplies in one pile, receipts somewhere else, assignment notes mixed in with packaging stuff, and then due dates are… like, written in three different places or not written at all. And then I look at everything and I don’t even know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll buy cute containers or notebooks thinking that’ll fix it, and then I just make a new pile next to the old pile. So yeah, I guess staying on task and organizing things feels like the bigger problem day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are as big of a problem for you as your trouble with organization and staying focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the organizing and focus stuff is still the bigger problem, but the restless thing is definitely there. Like even if I’m not actually going anywhere, I feel like my body is kind of buzzing? Like I need to move somehow.\n\nIt’s worse when I’m supposed to just sit still and listen, because then I’m suddenly super aware of my legs or my hands or like… the chair being uncomfortable, even if it’s probably not. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. So yeah, it is interfering.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do you feel your organizational difficulties and other symptoms are causing you?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I keep trying to tell myself it’s just annoying or I’ll fix it next week, but then it turns into actual consequences.\n\nLike with my program, I missed required steps—forms and things I was supposed to do in the right order—and now my financial aid is at risk, which makes me feel kind of sick when I think about it. And then with my shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not just frustrating. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced symptoms like disorganization, impulsivity, or restlessness for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My mom actually still has these old elementary school notes from teachers, which is kind of embarrassing that they still exist, but they say stuff like I was always getting out of my seat or talking before I got called on.\n\nAnd I guess I would rush through group activities too, even if I understood the work. Like I wasn’t necessarily confused, I’d just… go too fast or jump in. My mom brings it up sometimes when she’s looking through old papers, and then she’ll start talking about how she saved everything from school, like even random art projects, which is a whole other thing. But yeah, it seems like it was there pretty young.\n\nDoctor: Did you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t totally remember it clearly myself, because I was little, but my mom still has these notes from elementary school. And then I’ll start shifting around and trying to make it not obvious, but I feel like it is obvious.\n\nSo I don’t know if it’s as big as the organization thing, but it’s not nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… unfortunately, yeah. Like I keep wanting to say it’s just me being messy or behind, but it’s actually causing real problems now.\n\nWith my program, I missed some required steps—like forms or whatever things I was supposed to do in a certain order—and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really scary. And I hate dealing with that kind of office stuff because then I avoid it, and then it gets worse, and then I’m like, why did I do that?\n\nAnd with my shop stuff too, like people I’ve worked with on projects don’t really rely on me as much anymore. Which I get, because I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then I lose track of part of it, or I’m late getting back to them. It’s not like they’re mean about it, but I can tell they’re depending on me less. So yeah, it is interfering.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do you feel your organizational difficulties and other symptoms are causing you?\n\nPatient: A lot, honestly. I keep trying to tell myself it’s just annoying or I’ll fix it next week, but then it turns into actual consequences.\n\nLike with my program, I missed required steps—forms and things I was supposed to do in the right order—and now my financial aid is at risk, which makes me feel kind of sick when I think about it. And then with my shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not just frustrating. It’s stressful and embarrassing, and it makes me feel like I’m messing up things I actually care about.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced symptoms like disorganization, impulsivity, or restlessness for as long as you can remember, even as a young child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My mom actually still has these old elementary school notes from teachers, which is kind of embarrassing that they still exist, but they say stuff like I was always getting out of my seat or talking before I got called on.\n\nAnd I guess I would rush through group activities too, even if I understood the work. Like I wasn’t necessarily confused, I’d just… go too fast or jump in. My mom brings it up sometimes when she’s looking through old papers, and then she’ll start talking about how she saved everything from school, like even random art projects, which is a whole other thing. But yeah, it seems like it was there pretty young.\n\nDoctor: Did you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t totally remember it clearly myself, because I was little, but my mom still has these notes from elementary school. They say I was always out of my seat, talking before I got called on, and rushing through group activities even when I did understand the work.\n\nSo I guess it wasn’t always that I didn’t know what to do, it was more like I’d jump ahead or do it too fast. My mom keeps all that stuff in this box, like report cards and random class things—she has, like, every paper ever, which is kind of intense—but yeah, from the notes it sounds like teachers were bringing it up even back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity in all areas of your life, or are there certain situations where you seem to manage better?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of… everywhere, but it shows up differently depending on what I’m doing. Like with my program, it got really serious because I missed required steps—forms or whatever order things were supposed to happen in—and now my financial aid is at risk, which is awful.\n\nAnd with shop projects, that’s where I hate it because it affects other people. People have started depending on me less, because I don’t always keep track of what I said I’d do. I guess sometimes if something is really immediate or someone is standing right there, I manage better, but if it’s something I have to organize myself, it falls apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity consistently throughout your life, without any significant periods where these issues were absent?\n\nPatient: Not totally without breaks, I guess.
